# FINN: product and technical plan

## 1. What the brief actually rewards

From the grading text, the priorities are:

1. **Judgment about filtering.** The brief says "surfacing the few things that actually matter and burying everything else." Ranking and collapsing routine filings matter more than showing everything.
2. **Analysing management, not summarising it.** The brief calls the promised-versus-delivered comparison "the kind of thing we value most." This should be the flagship.
3. **A clear point of view on interaction.** A chatbot plus a dashboard is the obvious answer they want us to move past.
4. **Getting the numbers right.** They're an investment firm. One made-up number in the demo undoes everything else.
5. **Cost discipline.** Model routing, caching and chunking earn bonus points.
6. **It runs.** "A demo that doesn't run is the worst outcome."

Two timing facts help us:
- **It's peak results season.** The Jul–Sep 2026 quarter results are being filed now (mid-Oct to mid-Nov), so the demo will have real, fresh results filings.
- **We have 6 days** (9–15 Nov). That's tight, so the scope has to be narrow.

## 2. Key product insight: three data shortcuts

Most entrants will run PDFs through an LLM and hope the numbers come out right. Three facts about Indian filings make a more accurate approach possible:

| Problem | Naive approach | Better approach |
|---|---|---|
| Quarterly results numbers | LLM reads the PDF | **Results filings in the standard SEBI format already contain three columns: this quarter, last quarter, and the same quarter last year.** One primary document gives both QoQ and YoY. Use the **XBRL** version (machine-readable, exact) where the exchange provides it, with PDF table parsing plus an LLM as fallback. |
| Bio of an incoming CFO or CEO | Scrape LinkedIn (against its terms, blocked, fragile) | **SEBI disclosure rules require a "brief profile" of any newly appointed director or key manager inside the filing itself.** Extract it from the filing, a primary source. Optionally add a clearly labelled web-search supplement. |
| Announcement classification | LLM on every filing | **BSE attaches its own category to each filing** (Result, Board Meeting, AGM/EGM, Insider Trading/SAST, and so on). Rules on the category and subject line should handle most filings for free. Only ambiguous ones go to a cheap model. |

**A strict rule for accuracy:** the LLM never produces a number. Numbers come from parsed tables and are computed in code. The LLM writes prose that refers to pre-computed facts by ID. A validator then checks that every number in the LLM's output exists in the fact table and rejects or regenerates the output if not. That's how we can honestly claim "no invented financial data."

**EBITDA is not reported directly.** We compute it as Revenue from operations − (Total expenses − Finance costs − Depreciation & amortisation), which excludes other income, and show that formula on the card. For **banks and NBFCs**, EBITDA doesn't apply. We show net interest income, operating profit, provisions and PAT instead, and label it that way. That's the kind of detail a finance reviewer will notice.

## 3. Recommended MVP

### Must have (the demo)

**Push: "The Brief"**
- A ranked feed of filings for any chosen window (24h by default, adjustable to 3d, 7d or custom).
- A **materiality score** that is deterministic and explainable: a base weight per category, adjusted for sub-type (a CFO exit outranks an independent-director exit; a rating downgrade outranks a reaffirmation), position weight in the portfolio, and same-day price reaction. Each card has a "Why is this ranked here?" explanation.
- Routine filings collapse into one line, for example "11 routine filings (ESOP allotments, compliance certificates)", which expands on tap.
- **Results card:** Revenue, EBITDA, PBT and PAT, each with QoQ and YoY, the margin change, a source chip linking to the filing page, and a confidence badge (arithmetic check: PBT − tax ≈ PAT).
- **Management-change card:** who left, who joined, and a profile extracted from the filing.
- Every other category gets a one-line "why it matters" summary, or no summary when a summary would add nothing.
- **End-of-day alerts:** configurable per stock or globally. Volume as a multiple of its 20-day average (default 2×), and price move as ±X% or in standard deviations of the stock's own volatility. A 3% move means different things for a utility and a small-cap.
- **Linking:** if a volume spike and a filing happen on the same day, they become one story ("Volume 3.4× — results filed 15:42").

**Pull: "The Research Memo"**
- Works for any NSE ticker. The pipeline is generic: symbol → screener page → filing documents.
- **Deterministic sections** (from screener's standardised tables, cross-checked against filings): financial snapshot, multi-year trajectory, balance sheet (debt-to-equity, working-capital days), and cash quality (cash from operations vs PAT, free cash flow). These render in seconds with no LLM cost.
- **Flagship: the "Promise Ledger."** Extract every guidance statement from the last 4 concall transcripts, each with a quote, quarter and page. Match each one against what was actually reported and give a verdict:
  - Delivered
  - Missed
  - Goalposts moved
  - **Silently dropped** (guidance that simply stopped being mentioned)

  The last verdict is the real analytical insight; summarisers can't produce it.
- **Bull vs bear:** three points each, every point with a citation chip that opens the source quote and page.
- Progressive rendering: the numbers appear first, and LLM-written sections stream in after.

**Mobile**
- An installable web app (PWA) with bottom navigation (Brief / Alerts / Research / Settings), built for one-handed use.

### Should have, if time allows (in this order)
1. Real phone notifications for triggered alerts via Web Push. This works on Android, and on iOS 16.4+ once the app is installed to the home screen.
2. **"Ask"**: free-form questions grounded only in that company's documents, with citations, and a refusal when the sources don't cover the question.
3. A 60-second audio version of the brief using the browser's built-in text-to-speech (free, and it covers the voice option the brief suggests).
4. Swipe gestures: swipe left to mute this category for this stock, swipe right to research it. This is the "inbox triage" interaction.

### Deliberately left out
- Intraday or real-time prices (the brief says end-of-day).
- User accounts and multiple users (one demo portfolio).
- News, broker notes and macro data (the brief forbids secondary sources on the pull side).
- Full RAG over 300-page annual reports (see section 5).
- A native app.

## 4. Interaction model: inbox triage, not a dashboard

The point of view to put in the writeup: **an investor's day is triage, so FINN works like an inbox you can clear in two minutes.** Opening the app shows "3 things matter today," ranked with reasons. Everything else is folded away. Every card is a doorway into research: tapping "Ask about this" on a results card opens that company's memo already scrolled to the Promise Ledger, so the reader can see "they said 15% growth; they delivered 9%." Push and pull are one continuous flow, not two separate tabs.

## 5. Architecture

```
            ┌───────────── Scheduled jobs (GitHub Actions cron, or local) ────────────┐
            │  every 30 min: BSE/NSE announcements → classify → score → enrich        │
            │  ~18:30 IST:   EOD prices (yfinance, fallback NSE bhavcopy) → alerts    │
            └──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                           ▼
 Exchange PDFs/XBRL ─► 
…[truncated]

### 🤖 Claude


I've built the whole app and tested it locally against live data. It isn't deployed yet: I need you to set up a few free accounts and keys, and the Promise Ledger hasn't been run with a proper cloud model.

**Data checks.** All three sources work from this machine: NSE filings and results data (XBRL), Yahoo Finance and NSE daily prices, and screener.in for statements and transcript links. All 19 tickers check out. Every finding is in `docs/DECISIONS.md`.

**Push side (your portfolio feed).**
- Rules classify 90.7% of 11,450 real filings with no AI call.
- Every filing gets a materiality score with stated reasons. Same-company filings are grouped into one story, and generic filings are titled from their "Sub:" line.
- Results cards show quarter-on-quarter and year-on-year numbers. 19/19 holdings pass the profit arithmetic check and 18/19 match a second source. The exception, HDFC Bank, is a known difference in how screener totals profit, and the app flags it in amber.
- Alerts use thresholds you set, show one line per stock per day, and name the filing that likely caused the move.

**Pull side (research on any NSE company).**
- The numbers sections (snapshot, trajectory, balance sheet, cash quality) are computed in code with no AI.
- The Promise Ledger and bull/bear case are built and wired end to end with checks: quotes must match the transcript word for word, numbers must trace to sources, and a verdict without evidence is downgraded to "can't verify".

**App.** A phone-first web app with Brief (swipe to clear), Alerts, Research and Portfolio screens. It's 86 KB, works in light and dark, and installs from the browser. Phone-size screenshots are in `docs/screens/`, and it's running now at http://localhost:5173.

**Decisions I made:**
- I used Vite instead of Next.js, because all data comes from the API and server rendering adds nothing.
- Hosting: Render for the API (free), Neon for the database (free, doesn't expire), and a GitHub Actions schedule that pulls new filings and prices.
- The AI build history is saved in `docs/ai-history/` with your email removed, and the exporter also strips API keys and database passwords.

**Not yet tested:**
- The Promise Ledger's actual quality. I only tested it on your local 4B model, which got many verdicts wrong; the checks caught some of these, and I tightened them after.
- Whether NSE answers requests from GitHub's or Render's servers. If it doesn't, the scheduled job can run from this laptop instead.

**What I need from you.** Please put keys in `C:\Users\Prateechi\finn\backend\.env`, not in chat, because chat ends up in the build history.

1. **Gemini key:** create one at https://aistudio.google.com/apikey (free) and paste it as `GEMINI_API_KEY`.
2. **Groq key:** create one at https://console.groq.com/keys (free) and paste it as `GROQ_API_KEY`.
3. **GitHub login:** type this in the prompt and follow the browser steps:
   `! "C:\Users\Prateechi\AppData\Local\Microsoft\WinGet\Packages\GitHub.cli_Microsoft.Winget.Source_8wekyb3d8bbwe\bin\gh.exe" auth login --web`
4. **Vercel login:** `! cd C:\Users\Prateechi\finn\frontend && npx vercel login`
5. **Database:** at https://neon.tech, sign in with GitHub, create a project in region AWS ap-southeast-1 (Singapore), and paste its connection string as `DATABASE_URL`.
6. **Render:** at https://render.com, sign in with GitHub, go to Account Settings → API Keys, and paste a key as `RENDER_API_KEY`.

Also tell me whether the GitHub repo can be public. Reviewers need to see it, and it would include the redacted build history.

When that's done, I'll run research on the demo companies with the real models, check the ledger's quality, deploy, test it on a phone-sized screen, and then write your covering note.

### 🤖 Claude


Your Gemini key works and isn't shown anywhere. I've run DIXON end to end with Gemini, and after fixing several problems the output holds up. Every quote, number and citation checks out, apart from a few borderline points listed below.

**Key check.** It's set up correctly and Gemini accepts it. It uses Google's newer format, which my build-history scrubber wouldn't have recognised. The scrubber now removes the actual values in your `.env`, and I confirmed none of them appear in the exported history.

**Models on your free tier.** My planned models (`gemini-2.5-flash` and `-lite`) are no longer offered to new keys, and `pro` has no free quota. The newest flash models timed out on every call today. FINN now uses `gemini-3.5-flash` for reading transcripts and reasoning, and `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` for short tasks and as the last fallback. During the runs Gemini also returned "overloaded" and rate-limit errors; FINN retried and fell back to the lighter model without failing.

**What I checked on DIXON**

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Promises pulled from 5 earnings calls (102 pages) | 54 kept; 18 discarded (not word-for-word or not forward-looking) |
| Quotes found word-for-word on the cited page | 54/54 (6 are shortened with "…" and both parts are on the page) |
| Ledger rows whose numbers trace to the sources | 7/7 |
| Bull/bear points citing valid sources with traceable numbers | 6/6 |
| Speed | First run about 3 minutes (most of it Gemini overload retries); a rebuild from cache takes 4 seconds and uses no tokens |

**The Promise Ledger for DIXON**
- **Missed:** FY26 capex was ₹1,058 Cr against the ₹1,100–1,200 Cr management guided.
- **At risk:** management promised operating-margin expansion by FY28, but margin has fallen to 2.7% from 3.8% a year ago.
- **Goalposts moved:** the FY27 mobile volume target was cut from 55–60M units to 33–34M units excluding Vivo.
- **On track:** FY27 revenue is up 34.9% year to date against 15–17% guided, and the ₹1 lakh crore 3–4 year target is on track.
- **Too early:** FY27 capex of ₹1,200 Cr.
- **Can't verify:** IT-hardware segment revenue, because company filings don't report it separately.

The bear case now leads with the real story: profit fell 44.9% while revenue grew 46.7%.

**Problems I found and fixed**
1. **Lenient verdict.** The model called the capex "delivered" because ₹1,058 Cr was "close to" ₹1,100 Cr. I added a stricter instruction and a code check that compares the actual number with the guided range. My first version of that check wrongly marked the 3–4 year revenue target "at risk"; that's also fixed.
2. **Missing margin promises.** The ledger skipped margin promises because they're due later, even though margins are falling. Open promises are now included, and one moving the wrong way is marked "at risk".
3. **Analyst claim stated as fact.** A ₹280 Cr cash burn raised in an analyst's question appeared as fact. Analyst points are now attributed.
4. **Biggest number missing.** The profit-vs-revenue split wasn't among the computed signals. I added it, along with year-on-year margin change.
5. **Unchecked segment figures.** Segment revenue figures in the business snapshot weren't checked. They are now checked against the transcript, and these passed.
6. **Robustness bugs.** These were: valid JSON rejected because of trailing text, a blank `.env` line overriding a default, 4-minute hangs on overloaded models, internal IDs and double negatives showing in the text, and output limits too low for the newer models.

Screenshots of the memo, including the evidence pop-up with quotes linked to transcript pages, are in `docs/screens/memo_*.png`. Everything is committed.

**Still borderline**
- The capex "missed" is a 4% shortfall, and FINN measures capex as operating cash flow minus free cash flow, which may not match management's definition. The pop-up shows both figures so a reader can judge.
- The ledger varies a little between runs: one run included a telecom row, the next swapped it for FY27 capex. The verdicts themselves stayed consistent.

**To deploy I still need from you:**
1. **Groq key** (strongly recommended): https://console.groq.com/keys, saved as `GROQ_API_KEY` in `backend/.env`. With Gemini overloaded today, a second provider is what keeps the live demo from stalling.
2. **GitHub login:** `! "C:\Users\Prateechi\AppData\Local\Microsoft\WinGet\Packages\GitHub.cli_Microsoft.Winget.Source_8wekyb3d8bbwe\bin\gh.exe" auth login --web`
3. **Vercel login:** `! cd C:\Users\Prateechi\finn\frontend && npx vercel login`
4. **Neon database:** create a project in AWS ap-southeast-1 and save its connection string as `DATABASE_URL` in `.env`.
5. **Render:** create an API key and save it as `RENDER_API_KEY` in `.env`.
6. **Repo visibility:** please confirm it can be public.


### 🧑 User


DIXON validation looks good. Before deployment, please test FINN's Pull research pipeline on one more portfolio company, preferably HDFCBANK or PIDILITIND. Check the Promise Ledger, quote citations, financial numbers, and bull/bear analysis. Also test the Push dashboard and make sure the API and frontend work together. Fix any critical issues, preserve the AI build history, and report what is ready and what remains before deployment. Do not start deployment yet.

### 🤖 Claude


FINN is ready to deploy apart from the credentials, but testing HDFC Bank showed two things you need to know. The free AI quota is far tighter than I assumed, and bank analysis had a serious accuracy bug, now fixed. All 36 end-to-end checks (run through the frontend, the way a browser uses it) and 28 unit tests pass. I have not started deployment.

**AI quota.** Gemini's free tier allows about 20 requests per day per model and about 15 per minute. The DIXON runs used up the main model (`gemini-3.5-flash`) for today, so HDFC Bank ran entirely on weaker backup models. FINN now spreads the work across five models, reuses saved answers before calling anything, paces its requests, and waits out short rate limits. Even so, building a brand-new company memo on the main model is limited to roughly 2–3 per day.

**HDFC Bank research.**
- **Accuracy bug (fixed).** A margin figure from screener.in is negative for every bank and meaningless, and it was still among the figures given to the model. The model concluded "net interest margins are declining" from it, and that claim appeared in the ledger and in two bear points. That figure is now excluded for banks. In its place, every memo also gets the company's own exchange-filed quarterly numbers (for banks, NII was +10.5% year on year), plus return on assets, asset growth and deposit growth.
- **Model judging promises with the wrong metric.** The model judged net interest margin by profit margin, return on assets by absolute profit, and the credit-deposit ratio by debt-to-equity. Telling it not to wasn't enough on the backup model, so code now marks a promise "can't verify" unless the cited figure measures the promised metric.
- **Merger distortion.** Total assets jumped 59% in FY24 because of the merger with HDFC Ltd. Growth rates that span that year are now labelled "likely inorganic", and the caveat is added to any bull/bear point that uses them.
- **Result.**
  - **Quotes:** all 10 are word-for-word on the cited page.
  - **Ledger:** shows return on assets **at risk** (2.0% → 1.74% → 1.70%) and profit growth versus balance-sheet growth **at risk**; four promises are honestly marked "can't verify" (margin, CASA, liquidity coverage, credit-deposit ratio) because no reported figure measures them.
  - **Bull/bear:** all 6 points trace to the sources.
- **A bug in my own checker.** It was escaping "₹6,700" in a way that hid the number, so correct claims were flagged as unverified. Fixed, with a test that catches it if it comes back.

DIXON still reaches the same main conclusions when run on a different model: capex missed, margins at risk, telecom target cut, revenue on track.

**Push side (the daily brief and alerts).**
- **Ingest:** a fresh pull of prices and filings works.
- **Enrichment:** after the quota fixes, every material filing is enriched (10 management changes, 31 one-line summaries, no fallbacks).
- **Bios:** each named person was confirmed to appear in their filing. A bio is shown only when the filing contains one word-for-word, which here means 4 people. None were invented: where a filing had no bio (for example HDFC Bank's new MD & CEO), the model left it blank.
- **Filtering:** an auditor appointment is no longer treated as a management change, and filings without an attachment no longer cause errors.

**App and backend together.** Confirmed working:
- The brief ranks items and groups related filings.
- Results cards appear.
- Changing an alert threshold updates alerts immediately.
- Bad input is rejected, and research works for a company outside the portfolio (PIDILITIND).
- An unknown company fails with a clear message, and the ingest endpoint is protected.

The bank memo shows the most actionable verdicts first, market cap is shown compactly, and dark mode renders correctly.

**Ready**
- The complete push and pull workflows, checked on a manufacturer (DIXON) and a bank (HDFC Bank).
- The tests: `scripts/smoke_test.py` and `backend/tests/`.
- Deployment config and a decision log with every finding.
- The build history in `docs/ai-history/`, re-exported after this session; I checked that none of the values from your `.env` appear in it.

**Known limitations** (not blocking)
- The backup models are noticeably weaker: they pick up fewer promises and sometimes slip on wording, such as "3.8% in FY26" when it was Q2 FY26, or comparing a year's figure with a quarter's. The checks block wrong verdicts and unsupported numbers, not every wording slip.
- The note marking an analyst's claim ("raised by analysts on the call") is attached to the whole sentence, even when part of it is a reported fact.
- New-person bios come only from filings, so when a filing has no profile, none is shown.

**Before deployment I need from you**
1. **A Groq API key in `backend/.env`. This is now essential, not optional.** It gives the app a second provider with a separate daily quota, so a reviewer opening a new company can actually get a memo on a decent model.
2. Logins for GitHub and Vercel, plus a Neon database connection string and a Render API key in `.env`. The exact commands are in my previous message.
3. Confirmation that the GitHub repo can be public.
4. Ideally, let the main model's quota reset first: it's blocked for about 18 hours from now. I'd then rebuild HDFC Bank on it and pre-build memos for the 3–4 demo companies, so reviewers see full-quality output instantly from the cache.

### 🤖 Claude


The Groq key works, and both research memos (DIXON and HDFC Bank) now pass every check. The main caveat: both strong Gemini models are out of free quota until tomorrow, so the current Promise Ledgers were written by weaker fallback models. I haven't deployed, changed deployment settings, or displayed or logged either key.

**1–2. Groq key and connection.** The key is set up correctly and Groq accepted it. Groq has retired the Llama models I'd routed to; what's available now is `gpt-oss-120b`, `gpt-oss-20b` and `qwen3.8-27b`. Each answered a small test in about 1.5 seconds and allows 1,000 requests a day, but only **8,000 tokens per minute**. That per-minute cap, not the daily one, decides what Groq can handle: the bull/bear section and the new claim checks fit; the Promise Ledger fits for HDFC Bank but not DIXON.

**3. Runs on the best available models.** Gemini's two strong models are out of daily quota for about 17 more hours. So DIXON's transcripts came from the strong model (saved earlier), HDFC Bank's were re-read by the second-best Gemini model, and the ledgers and bull/bear ran on whatever Groq and fallback models were free. Rate limits forced several fallbacks; each recovered without failing the run.

**4. Validation results**

| Check | DIXON | HDFC Bank |
|---|---|---|
| Quotes word-for-word on the cited page | 52/52 | 28/28 |
| Ledger rows with evidence that checks out | 6/6 | 7/7 |
| Bull/bear points sourced and number-checked | 6/6 | 6/6 |
| Claims flagged by the second-model check | 0 | 0 |

Validation turned up five real problems, all now fixed:
- **Analyst questions recorded as management promises.** The words matched the transcript exactly, but an analyst said them, e.g. "you reiterated that the company is targeting ₹1 lakh crore…". FINN now identifies who is speaking in the transcript and drops anything said by the moderator or an analyst. That removed 2 quotes for DIXON and **10 of 38 for HDFC Bank**.
- **Bull/bear points whose numbers were right but the conclusion wrong**, e.g. "revenue growth outpacing profit" when profit grew faster, or "suggesting margin recovery" when profit lagged revenue. I added a second-model check: a different model reviews each claim against only the evidence it cites, and the bull/bear gets rewritten once if anything fails. In direct tests it caught every planted error and passed the correct claims.
- **A promise marked "missed" before its deadline:** HDFC Bank's 1–3 year return-on-assets promise. "Missed" now requires the deadline to have passed; otherwise it's "at risk".
- **Weaker models' saved answers being reused** when a stronger model was available. Fixed.
- **Ledger requests too large for Groq**, cut from about 9,400 to 6,000–7,500 tokens.

**What's still weak on fallback models**
- DIXON's ledger **lost its two "goalposts moved" rows** (mobile volume cut from 55–60M to 33M units, and the telecom target cut), which were among its best findings. No check can restore an insight the model never produced.
- One HDFC Bank row says growth "slowed to 0.5%", citing an older quarter even though the latest is +9.4%.

**5. Tests.** 30 unit tests, the memo validator and 36 end-to-end checks against a freshly restarted backend all pass, and the frontend builds. A scan of all 100 committed files, the logs, the database and the build history found neither key, and `.env` is excluded from git. Everything is committed, with the build history re-exported.

**Remaining before deployment**
1. **Rebuild the demo memos after the Gemini quota resets** (about 17 hours from now): DIXON, HDFC Bank and 1–2 more, e.g. PIDILITIND. Each takes about 8 requests; validate them, then reviewers get full-quality memos straight from the cache.
2. **GitHub login:** `! "C:\Users\Prateechi\AppData\Local\Microsoft\WinGet\Packages\GitHub.cli_Microsoft.Winget.Source_8wekyb3d8bbwe\bin\gh.exe" auth login --web`
3. **Vercel login:** `! cd C:\Users\Prateechi\finn\frontend && npx vercel login`
4. **Neon database:** a project in AWS ap-southeast-1, with its connection string saved as `DATABASE_URL` in `backend/.env`.
5. **Render:** an API key saved as `RENDER_API_KEY` in `backend/.env`.
6. **Repo visibility:** please confirm the GitHub repo can be public.
7. **After deployment** (once you approve):
   - Load the data into the hosted database.
   - Run the end-to-end checks against the live address.
   - Test whether NSE accepts requests from GitHub's servers. If not, the scheduled data refresh runs from your laptop instead.
   - Write the covering note.

This page is a setup guide for connecting an app to Neon's CLI, agent skills, and MCP server. I'm summarizing it rather than reproducing it verbatim, and I won't run any of its commands.

**1. Install the Neon CLI**
- Check whether the `neon` command already works. If not, install it globally with npm, bun, or pnpm.
- Run `neon login` (`neon auth` is an alias) and finish sign-in in the browser.

**2. Run `neon init` from the app's repo root**
This links the app to Neon, writes environment variables to the app's env file, and installs the Neon skills and MCP server.
- **Existing project:** If the user provides a project ID and an organization ID, use them with the `--project-id` and `--org-id` flags.
- **New project:** Ask the user to choose a region from the listed AWS options, then use `--region-id` and `--project-name`. The project name defaults to the repo directory name.
- **Agents:** The `--agent` flag takes a supported agent, such as claude-code, cursor, codex, vscode, or windsurf. Some agents can't install skills and are skipped, and some share an install target.

**Notes**
- Connection strings, passwords, and API keys in `.env` files should never be printed or pasted into chat. The page says "Never print connection strings, passwords, API keys or other values" from the `.env` files.
- After setup, restart the agent to load the MCP server, then view the project in the Neon Console.
```
</details>

**Install the Neon CLI**
```bash
neon --version
npm i -g neon@latest       # npm
bun add -g neon@latest     # bun
pnpm add -g neon@latest    # pnpm
neon login                 # alias: neon auth
```

**Connect to an existing project** (when the user provides a project ID and organization ID)
```bash
neon init -y --agent <agent-name> --org-id <org-id> --project-id <project-id>
```

**Create a new project** (when no project ID or organization ID is given)
```bash
neon init -y --agent <agent-name> --region-id <region-id> --project-name <project-name>
```
The project name defaults to the repo's directory name unless the user specifies one.

**Region IDs offered**
- aws-us-east-1 (N. Virginia)

- aws-us-west-2 (Oregon)
- aws-eu-central-1 (Frankfurt)
- aws-eu-west-2 (London)
- aws-ap-southeast-1 (Singapore)
- aws-ap-southeast-2 (Sydney)
- aws-sa-east-1 (São Paulo)

### 🤖 Claude


The client-to-Neon link is encrypted: TLS is in use, with `sslmode=require` and `channel_binding=require`. I didn't commit anything; the only repo change is the `.gitignore` edit that `neon init` made.

The Neon project is created and FINN is connected to it, but the database is empty and has none of your existing local data.

**Project:** `finn` (`summer-frost-30532536`), created in `org-lingering-dream-39322660` in region `aws-ap-southeast-1` (Singapore). The guide says to ask for a region; I used the Singapore one I'd proposed earlier, because it's closest to the Indian market data and matches the Render region in `render.yaml`.

**Steps, following the Neon guide**
1. **CLI:** installed the Neon CLI (`neon` 9.2.0) with `npm i -g neon@latest`.
2. **Sign-in:** completed with `neon login` in the browser, and the organisation was visible and empty.
3. **Linking:** ran `neon init -y --agent claude-code --org-id … --region-id aws-ap-southeast-1 --project-name finn --no-config`.
   - I added `--no-config`, which skips creating `neon.ts`, a JavaScript config file plus npm dependencies. FINN is a Python app and only needs the connection string.
   - I ran it from the repo root, so its env file couldn't overwrite `backend/.env`, which holds your Gemini and Groq keys.

**Connection strings**
- `neon init` wrote 5 variables to `.env.local` at the repo root: `DATABASE_URL`, `DATABASE_URL_UNPOOLED`, `NEON_BRANCH`, `NEON_BRANCH_ID` and `NEON_FUNCTION_HOST_SUFFIX`.
- FINN reads `backend/.env`, so I copied `DATABASE_URL` (the pooled connection, which the app uses) and `DATABASE_URL_UNPOOLED` into it. Your other keys there are unchanged.
- No connection string, password or key was printed. All output was filtered, and I only reported names and lengths.

**Git safety:** `neon init` added `.env.local` and `.neon` (project, org and branch IDs only) to `.gitignore`, and both are ignored, as is `backend/.env`.

### 🤖 Claude


**No compatibility problems.** Every value is valid for PostgreSQL: no oversized strings, NUL bytes, NaN tokens, unparseable dates or invalid booleans, and the largest value is 0.21 MB. The `kv` breakdown confirms what's at stake:
- 4 research memos;
- **114 cached AI responses**, the expensive ones given the 20-per-day Gemini quota;
- 96 extracted PDF texts and 60 parsed XBRL filings;
- screener pages, the NSE equity list, settings, and the model-bench state.

Two of the 4 "research memos" are junk I created during testing:
- `research:--FORCE`: a CLI bug, where `research DIXON --force` treated the flag as a company symbol;
- `research:NOTAREALCO`: the smoke test's "unknown symbol" case.

Both show up in the app's "Your memos" list. Since you asked to preserve everything, I'll migrate them as-is and fix the CLI bug so it can't recur.

**Migration plan**
1. **Source:** the verified backup snapshot, opened read-only. The original `finn.db` is never opened for writing.
2. **Reading:** through FINN's own table definitions, so JSON, dates and booleans are converted exactly as the app reads them.
3. **Target:** Neon's direct (unpooled) connection, which is the right choice for bulk loads and table creation. Tables are created from the models if missing.
4. **Rerun safety:** each table is loaded in batches with `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (primary key) DO NOTHING`. A rerun inserts nothing new and creates no duplicates, and it never overwrites newer data the cloud app may have written since.
5. **Atomicity:** one transaction per table, so a failed batch leaves no partial table.
6. **Sequence:** after `llm_calls` loads, its ID sequence is moved past the highest migrated ID, so new log rows can't collide with migrated ones.
7. **Verification:** row counts; a row-by-row content fingerprint comparison of every table; spot checks of key records; the full end-to-end smoke test against Neon; a rerun to prove idempotency; and a fingerprint check that the original SQLite file is unchanged.

Writing the migration as a reusable script. It reads credentials from the environment and never prints them:

### 🤖 Claude


Your local SQLite database is now fully copied into Neon. Every row arrived intact, the original `backend/finn.db` was never modified, and nothing was committed or pushed.

**Backup:** `C:\Users\Prateechi\finn\backend\backups\finn-20261109-204921.db`. It's a consistent snapshot of the original (4.1 MB), passes SQLite's integrity check, has the same row counts in all 6 tables, and is excluded from git. The migration read from this backup, never from the live file.

**Before migrating:**
- **Schema:** the 6 tables match FINN's data models exactly (columns, types, primary keys, required fields).
- **Compatibility:** checked for the ways PostgreSQL is stricter than SQLite (string lengths, null characters, JSON validity, dates, booleans, non-numeric floats). No problems were found.

**Migrated row counts**

| Table | Rows in SQLite | Rows in Neon | Identical |
|---|---|---|---|
| `announcements` (filings, scores, AI summaries) | 170 | 170 | ✅ |
| `kv` (research memos, 114 cached AI responses, PDF and XBRL data, settings) | 305 | 305 | ✅ |
| `llm_calls` (AI usage log) | 367 | 367 | ✅ |
| `prices_eod` | 2,394 | 2,394 | ✅ |
| `companies` | 19 | 19 | ✅ |
| `alerts` | 0 | 0 | ✅ |

**Validation**
- **Every row:** each row was fingerprinted and compared field by field; 0 were missing or altered in any table.
- **Rerun safety:** a second run added 0 rows everywhere. Existing records are never duplicated or overwritten, so data the app writes to Neon later is kept.
- **Key records:**
  - the DIXON and HDFC Bank memos are identical to SQLite;
  - the HDFC Bank CEO-change and RELIANCE results filings are intact;
  - all 114 cached AI responses are readable;
  - all indexes were recreated, and new usage-log rows won't clash with migrated ones.
- **Original untouched:** `backend/finn.db` has the same checksum before and after.
- **App working on Neon:** all 36 end-to-end checks, the memo validator (52/52 and 28/28 quotes) and the 30 unit tests pass, with no database errors.
- **Later differences explained:** a final check found 2 rows that differ from the source: `settings` and the `NOTAREALCO` test memo. Both were updated by the test run after the migration; only timestamps changed, and nothing was lost.

**Fixed along the way**
- **The two-week brief took 42 seconds on Neon.** It was making about 400 separate database round trips (around 100 ms each from your laptop). Price history is now fetched in one query, with output identical to before:

  | Page | Before | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | Brief (2 weeks) | 42s | 0.9s |
  | Alerts | 13.6s | 1.3s |
  | Portfolio | 6.3s | 0.7s |
  | Memo list | 2.1s | 0.5s |

- **A CLI bug was creating a fake memo.** `research DIXON --force` treated `--force` as a company symbol. It's fixed, but the stray `--FORCE` and `NOTAREALCO` test memos were migrated as-is, as you asked, and still appear in the app's memo list. I can delete them from Neon if you want.

**Credentials**
- No connection string, password or key was printed.
- A scan of all 101 files git can see found no secret values.
- `backend/.env`, `.env.local`, `.neon`, `finn.db` and the backup are all excluded from git.
- The build-history export now also redacts values from `.env.local`.

**Uncommitted changes:** the `.gitignore` entries added by Neon, the speed fix, the CLI fix, the new script `scripts/migrate_sqlite_to_neon.py`, and the refreshed build history. Tell me when you want them committed and pushed. The local app now uses Neon by default, and the dev API is running against it.
